end

procedure

Theorem D.20
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, let
k\geq 1
, let
U_{B},V_{B},W_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
denote a rank-
k
,
\alpha
-approximation to
A
. Then there exists an algorithm which takes
O(\nnz(A))+O(n^{2})\poly(k)
time and outputs three matrices:
C\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times c}
with columns from
A
,
R\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
with rows from
A
,
T\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times t}
with tubes from
A
, and a tensor
U\in\mathbb{R}^{c\times r\times t}
with
\rank(U)=k
such that
c=r=t=O(k\log k)
, and

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{c}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{t}U_{i,j,l}\cdot C_{i}\otimes R_{j}\otimes T_{l}-A\right\|_{1}\leq\widetilde{O}(k^{1.5})\alpha\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{1}

holds with probability
9/10
.

Proof.

We define

\displaystyle\OPT:=\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{1}.

We already have three matrices
U_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
V_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
W_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and these three matrices provide a
\rank
-
k
,
\alpha
approximation to
A
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(U_{B})_{i}\otimes(V_{B})_{i}\otimes(W_{B})_{i}-A\right\|_{1}\leq\alpha\OPT

(40)

Let
B_{1}=V_{B}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
denote the matrix where the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
(V_{B})_{i}\otimes(W_{B})_{i}
. By Section B.3, we can compute
D_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
which is a sampling and rescaling matrix corresponding to the Lewis weights of
B_{1}^{\top}
in
O(n^{2}\poly(k))
time, and there are
d_{1}=O(k\log k)
nonzero entries on the diagonal of
D_{1}
. Let
A_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{2}}
denote the matrix obtained by flattening
A
along the
i
-th direction, for each
i\in[3]
.

Define
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|UB_{1}-A_{1}\|_{1}
,
\widehat{U}=A_{1}D_{1}(B_{1}D_{1})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
V_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|V\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})-A_{2}\|_{1}
, and
U^{\prime}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|UB_{1}D_{1}-A_{1}D_{1}\|_{1}
.

By Claim
B.13
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}B_{1}D_{1}-A_{1}D_{1}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{1}}\|U^{\prime}B_{1}D_{1}-A_{1}D_{1}\|_{1}

Due to Lemma D.11 and Lemma D.8 (in
[
SWZ17
]
) with constant probability, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{1}}\alpha_{D_{1}}\|U^{*}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{1},

(41)

where
\alpha_{D_{1}}=O(1)
.

Recall that
(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
denotes the matrix where the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
\widehat{U}_{i}\otimes(W_{B})_{i}
,
\forall i\in[k]
. Now, we can show,

\displaystyle\|V_{0}\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})-A_{2}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{U}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }V_{0}=\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|V\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})-A_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{d_{1}}\|U^{*}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{1}

by Equation (
41
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{d_{1}}\|U_{B}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }U^{*}=\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|UB_{1}-A_{1}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O(\sqrt{d_{1}})\alpha\OPT

by Equation (
40
)

(42)

We define
B_{2}=\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top}
. We can compute
D_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
which is a sampling and rescaling matrix corresponding to the Lewis weights of
B_{2}^{\top}
in
O(n^{2}\poly(k))
time, and there are
d_{2}=O(k\log k)
nonzero entries on the diagonal of
D_{2}
.

Define
V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution of
\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|VB_{2}-A_{2}\|_{1}
,
\widehat{V}=A_{2}D_{2}(B_{2}D_{2})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
W_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution of
\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|W\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})-A_{3}\|_{1}
, and
V^{\prime}
to be the optimal solution of
\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|VB_{2}D_{2}-A_{2}D_{2}\|_{1}
.

By Claim
B.13
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{V}B_{2}D_{2}-A_{2}D_{2}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{2}}\|V^{\prime}B_{2}D_{2}-A_{2}D_{2}\|_{1}.

Due to Lemma D.11 and Lemma D.8(in
[
SWZ17
]
) with constant probability, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{V}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{2}}\alpha_{D_{2}}\|V^{*}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{1},

(43)

where
\alpha_{D_{2}}=O(1)
.

Recall that
(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
denotes the matrix for which the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
\widehat{U}_{i}\otimes\widehat{V}_{i}
,
\forall i\in[k]
. Now, we can show,

\displaystyle\|W_{0}\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})-A_{3}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{V}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }W_{0}=\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|W\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})-A_{3}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{d_{2}}\|V^{*}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{1}

by Equation (
43
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{d_{2}}\|V_{0}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }V^{*}=\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|VB_{2}-A_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O(\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}})\alpha\OPT

by Equation (
D.7
)

(44)

We define
B_{3}=\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top}
. We can compute
D_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
which is a sampling and rescaling matrix corresponding to the Lewis weights of
B_{3}^{\top}
in
O(n^{2}\poly(k))
time, and there are
d_{3}=O(k\log k)
nonzero entries on the diagonal of
D_{3}
.

Define
W^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution to
\min_{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|WB_{3}-A_{3}\|_{1}
,
\widehat{W}=A_{3}D_{3}(B_{3}D_{3})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
and
W^{\prime}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|WB_{3}D_{3}-A_{3}D_{3}\|_{1}
.

By Claim
B.13
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}B_{3}D_{3}-A_{3}D_{3}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{3}}\|W^{\prime}B_{3}D_{3}-A_{3}D_{3}\|_{1}.

Due to Lemma D.11 and Lemma D.8(in
[
SWZ17
]
) with constant probability, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{3}}\alpha_{D_{3}}\|W^{*}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{1},

(45)

where
\alpha_{D_{3}}=O(1)
. Now we can show,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{d_{3}}\|W^{*}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{1},

by Equation (
45
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{d_{3}}\|W_{0}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{1},

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }W^{*}=\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|WB_{3}-A_{3}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O(\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}})\alpha\OPT

by Equation (
D.7
)

Thus, it implies,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\widehat{U}_{i}\otimes\widehat{V}_{i}\otimes\widehat{W}_{i}-A\right\|_{1}\leq\poly(k,\log n)\OPT.

where
\widehat{U}=A_{1}D_{1}(B_{1}D_{1})^{\dagger}
,
\widehat{V}=A_{2}D_{2}(B_{2}D_{2})^{\dagger}
,
\widehat{W}=A_{3}D_{3}(B_{3}D_{3})^{\dagger}
.

∎

Theorem D.21
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
, there exists an algorithm which takes
O(\nnz(A))+O(n^{2})\poly(k)+2^{\widetilde{O}(k^{2})}
time and outputs three matrices
C\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times c}
with columns from
A
,
R\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
with rows from
A
,
T\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times t}
with tubes from
A
, and a tensor
U\in\mathbb{R}^{c\times r\times t}
with
\rank(U)=k
such that
c=r=t=O(k\log k)
, and

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{c}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{t}U_{i,j,l}\cdot C_{i}\otimes R_{j}\otimes T_{l}-A\right\|_{1}\leq\poly(k,\log n)\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}{\min}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{1},

holds with probability
9/10
.

Proof.

This follows by combining Corollary
D.18
and Theorem
D.20
.
∎

Appendix E
Entry-wise
\ell_{p}
Norm for Arbitrary Tensors,
1<p<2

There is a long line of research dealing with
\ell_{p}
norm-related problems
[
DDH+09
,
MM13
,
CDMI+13
,
CP15
,
BCKY16
,
YCRM16
,
BBC+17
]
.

In this section, we provide several different algorithms for tensor
\ell_{p}
-low rank approximation. Section
E.1
formally states the
\ell_{p}
version of Theorem C.1 in
[
SWZ17
]
. Section
E.2
presents several existence results. Section
E.3
describes a tool that is able to reduce the size of the objective function from
\poly(n)
to
\poly(k)
. Section
E.4
discusses the case when the problem size is small. Section
E.5
provides several bicriteria algorithms.
Section
E.6
summarizes a batch of algorithms. Section
E.7
provides an algorithm for
\ell_{p}
norm CURT decomposition.

Notice that if the
\rank
-
k
solution does not exist, then every bicriteria algorithm in Section
E.5
can be stated in the form as Theorem
1.1
, and every algorithm which can output a
\rank
-
k
solution in Section
E.6
can be stated in the form as Theorem
1.2
. See Section
1
for more details.

E.1
Existence results for matrix case

Theorem E.1

(
[
SWZ17
]
)
.

Let
1\leq p<2
. Given
V\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n},A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
. Let
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s}
be a proper random sketching matrix. Let

\displaystyle\widehat{U}=\arg\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}}\|UVS-AS\|_{F}^{2},

i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{U}=AS(VS)^{\dagger}.

Then with probability at least
0.999,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}V-A\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\cdot\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}}\|UV-A\|_{p}^{p}.

\mathrm{(I)}
.
S
denotes a dense
p
-stable transform,

s=\widetilde{O}(k)
,
\alpha=\widetilde{O}(k^{1-p/2})\log d
.

\mathrm{(II)}
.
S
denotes a sparse
p
-stable transform,

s=\widetilde{O}(k^{5})
,
\alpha=\widetilde{O}(k^{5-5p/2+2/p})\log d
.

\mathrm{(III)}
.
S^{\top}
denotes a sampling/rescaling matrix according to the
\ell_{p}
Lewis weights of
V^{\top}
,

s=\widetilde{O}(k)
,
\alpha=\widetilde{O}(k^{1-p/2})
.

We give the proof for completeness.

Proof.

Let
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s}
be a sketching matrix which satisfies the property
(*)
:
\forall c\geq 1,\widetilde{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
which satisfy

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}VS-AS\|_{p}^{p}\leq c\cdot\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}}\|UVS-AS\|_{p}^{p},

we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}V-A\|_{p}^{p}\leq c\beta_{S}\cdot\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}}\|UV-A\|_{p}^{p},

where
\beta_{S}\geq 1
only depends on the sketching matrix
S
. Let

\displaystyle\forall i\in[d],(\widehat{U}^{i})^{\top}=\arg\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\|x^{\top}VS-A^{i}S\|_{2}^{2},

i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{U}=AS(VS)^{\dagger}.

Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}=\arg\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}}\|UVS-AS\|_{p}^{p}.

Then, we have:

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{U}VS-AS\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{d}\|\widehat{U}^{i}VS-A^{i}S\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{d}(s^{1/p-1/2}\|\widehat{U}^{i}VS-A^{i}S\|_{2})^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{d}(s^{1/p-1/2}\|\widetilde{U}^{i}VS-A^{i}S\|_{2})^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{d}(s^{1/p-1/2}\|\widetilde{U}^{i}VS-A^{i}S\|_{p})^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ s^{1-p/2}\|\widetilde{U}VS-AS\|_{p}^{p}.

The first inequality follows using
\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{s},\|x\|_{p}\leq s^{1/p-1/2}\|x\|_{2}
since
p<2
. The third inequality follows using
\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{s},\|x\|_{2}\leq\|x\|_{p}
since
p<2
. Thus, according to the property
(*)
of
S
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}V-A\|_{p}^{p}\leq s^{1-p/2}\beta_{S}\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}}\|UV-A\|_{p}^{p}.

Due to Lemma E.8 and Lemma E.11 of
[
SWZ17
]
, we have:

for (I),
s=\widetilde{O}(k),\beta_{S}=O(\log d),\alpha=s^{1-p/2}\beta_{S}=\widetilde{O}(k^{1-p/2})\log d
,

for (II),
s=\widetilde{O}(k^{5}),\beta_{S}=\widetilde{O}(k^{2/p}\log d),\alpha=s^{1-p/2}\beta_{S}=\widetilde{O}(k^{5-5p/2+2/p})\log d
,

for (III),
s=\widetilde{O}(k),\beta_{S}=O(1),\alpha=s^{1-p/2}\beta_{S}=\widetilde{O}(k^{1-p/2})
.
∎

E.2
Existence results

Theorem E.2
.

Given a 3rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
,
there exist three matrices
S_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{1}}
,
S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{2}}
,
S_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{3}}
such that

\displaystyle\min_{X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(A_{1}S_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes(A_{2}S_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes(A_{3}S_{3}X_{3})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ A_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}}{\min}\|A_{k}-A\|_{p}^{p},

holds with probability
99/100
.

\mathrm{(I)}
. Using a dense
p
-stable transform,

s_{1}=s_{2}=s_{3}=\widetilde{O}(k)
,
\alpha=\widetilde{O}(k^{3-1.5p})\log^{3}n
.

\mathrm{(II)}
. Using a sparse
p
-stable transform,

s_{1}=s_{2}=s_{3}=\widetilde{O}(k^{5})
,
\alpha=\widetilde{O}(k^{15-7.5p+6/p})\log^{3}n
.

\mathrm{(III)}
. Guessing Lewis weights,

s_{1}=s_{2}=s_{3}=\widetilde{O}(k)
,
\alpha=\widetilde{O}(k^{3-1.5p})
.

Proof.

We use
\OPT
to denote

\displaystyle\OPT:=\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ A_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}}{\min}\|A_{k}-A\|_{p}^{p}.

Given a tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}\times n_{3}}
, we define three matrices
A_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}n_{3}},A_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}\times n_{3}n_{1}},A_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{3}\times n_{1}n_{2}}
such that, for any
i\in[n_{1}],j\in[n_{2}],l\in[n_{3}]

\displaystyle A_{i,j,l}=(A_{1})_{i,(j-1)\cdot n_{3}+l}=(A_{2})_{j,(l-1)\cdot n_{1}+i}=({A}_{3})_{l,(i-1)\cdot n_{2}+j}.

We fix
V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
W^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
, and use
V_{1}^{*},V_{2}^{*},\cdots,V_{k}^{*}
to denote the columns of
V^{*}
and
W_{1}^{*},W_{2}^{*},\cdots,W_{k}^{*}
to denote the columns of
W^{*}
.

We consider the following optimization problem,

\displaystyle\min_{U_{1},\cdots,U_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}U_{i}\otimes V_{i}^{*}\otimes W_{i}^{*}-A\right\|_{p}^{p},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\min_{U_{1},\cdots,U_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}U_{1}&U_{2}&\cdots&U_{k}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}V_{1}^{*}\otimes W_{1}^{*}\\
V_{2}^{*}\otimes W_{2}^{*}\\
\cdots\\
V_{k}^{*}\otimes W_{k}^{*}\end{bmatrix}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}.

We use matrix
Z_{1}
to denote
V^{*\top}\odot W^{*\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
and matrix
U
to denote
\begin{bmatrix}U_{1}&U_{2}&\cdots&U_{k}\end{bmatrix}
. Then we can obtain the following equivalent objective function,

\displaystyle\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|UZ_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}.

Choose a sketching matrix (a dense
p
-stable, a sparse
p
-stable or an
\ell_{p}
Lewis weight sampling/rescaling matrix to
Z_{1}
)
S_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{1}}
. We can obtain the optimization problem,

\displaystyle\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|UZ_{1}S_{1}-A_{1}S_{1}\|_{p}^{p}=\min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|U^{i}Z_{1}S_{1}-(A_{1}S_{1})^{i}\|_{p}^{p},

where
U^{i}
denotes the
i
-th row of matrix
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
(A_{1}S_{1})^{i}
denotes the
i
-th row of matrix
A_{1}S_{1}
. Instead of solving it under the
\ell_{p}
-norm, we consider the
\ell_{2}
-norm relaxation,

\displaystyle\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|UZ_{1}S_{1}-A_{1}S_{1}\|_{F}^{2}=\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|U^{i}Z_{1}S_{1}-(A_{1}S_{1})^{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Let
\widehat{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
denote the optimal solution of the above optimization problem. Then,
\widehat{U}=A_{1}S_{1}(Z_{1}S_{1})^{\dagger}
. We plug
\widehat{U}
into the objective function under the
\ell_{p}
-norm. By choosing
s_{1}
and by the properties of sketching matrices (a dense
p
-stable, a sparse
p
-stable or an
\ell_{p}
Lewis weight sampling/rescaling matrix to
Z_{1}
)
S_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}Z_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|UZ_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}=\alpha\OPT.

This implies

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}\otimes V^{*}\otimes W^{*}-A\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\OPT.

As a second step, we fix
\widehat{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
W^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
, and convert tensor
A
into matrix
A_{2}
. Let matrix
Z_{2}
denote
\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W^{*\top}
. We consider the following objective function,

\displaystyle\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|VZ_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p},

and the optimal cost of it is at most
\alpha\OPT
.

We choose a sketching matrix (a dense
p
-stable, a sparse
p
-stable or an
\ell_{p}
Lewis weight sampling/rescaling matrix to
Z_{2}
)
S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{2}}
and sketch on the right of the objective function to obtain the new objective function,

\displaystyle\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|VZ_{2}S_{2}-A_{2}S_{2}\|_{p}^{p}=\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|V^{i}Z_{2}S_{2}-(A_{2}S_{2})^{i}\|_{p}^{p},

where
V^{i}
denotes the
i
-th row of matrix
V
and
(A_{2}S_{2})^{i}
denotes the
i
-th row of matrix
A_{2}S_{2}
. Instead of solving this under the
\ell_{p}
-norm, we consider the
\ell_{2}
-norm relaxation,

\displaystyle\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|VZ_{2}S_{2}-A_{2}S_{2}\|_{F}^{2}=\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|V^{i}(Z_{2}S_{2})-(A_{2}S_{2})^{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Let
\widehat{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
denote the optimal solution of the above problem. Then
\widehat{V}=A_{2}S_{2}(Z_{2}S_{2})^{\dagger}
. By properties of sketching matrix
S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{2}}
, we have,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{V}Z_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|VZ_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha^{2}\OPT,

which implies

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}\otimes\widehat{V}\otimes W^{*}-A\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha^{2}\OPT,

As a third step, we fix the matrices
\widehat{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
\widehat{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
. We can convert tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
into matrix
A_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n}
. Let matrix
Z_{3}
denote
\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
. We consider the following objective function,

\displaystyle\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|WZ_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p},

and the optimal cost of it is at most
\alpha^{2}\OPT
.

We choose sketching matrix (a dense
p
-stable, a sparse
p
-stable or an
\ell_{p}
Lewis weight sampling/rescaling matrix to
Z_{3}
)
S_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{3}}
and sketch on the right of the objective function to obtain the new objective function,

\displaystyle\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|WZ_{3}S_{3}-A_{3}S_{3}\|_{p}^{p}.

Instead of solving this under the
\ell_{p}
-norm, we consider the
\ell_{2}
-norm relaxation,

\displaystyle\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|WZ_{3}S_{3}-A_{3}S_{3}\|_{F}^{2}=\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|W^{i}(Z_{3}S_{3})-(A_{3}S_{3})^{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Let
\widehat{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
denote the optimal solution of the above problem. Then
\widehat{W}=A_{3}S_{3}(Z_{3}S_{3})^{\dagger}
. By properties of sketching matrix
S_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{3}}
, we have,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}Z_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|WZ_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha^{3}\OPT.

Thus, we obtain,

\displaystyle\min_{X_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times k},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{2}\times k},X_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{3}\times k}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(A_{1}S_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes(A_{2}S_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes(A_{3}S_{3}X_{3})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha^{3}\OPT.

According to Theorem
E.1
, we let
s=s_{1}=s_{2}=s_{3}
and take the corresponding
\alpha
. We can directly get the results for (I), (II) and (III).
∎

E.3
Polynomial in
k
size reduction

Definition E.3

(Definition E.1 in
[
SWZ17
]
)
.

Given a matrix
M\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d},
if matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|SM\|_{p}^{p}\leq\beta\|M\|_{p}^{p},

then
S
has at most
\beta
dilation on
M
in the
\ell_{p}
case.

Definition E.4

(Definition E.2 in
[
SWZ17
]
)
.

Given a matrix
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k},
if matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{k},\|SUx\|_{p}^{p}\geq\frac{1}{\beta}\|Ux\|_{p}^{p},

then
S
has at most
\beta
contraction on
U
in the
\ell_{p}
case.

Theorem E.5
.

Given a tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}\times n_{3}}
and three matrices
V_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times b_{1}},V_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}\times b_{2}},V_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{3}\times b_{3}},
let
X_{1}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X_{2}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X_{3}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}
satisfy

\displaystyle X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*},X_{3}^{*}=\underset{X_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}}{\arg\min}\|V_{1}X_{1}\otimes V_{2}X_{2}\otimes V_{3}X_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}.

Let
S\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
have at most
\beta_{1}\geq 1
dilation on
V_{1}X_{1}^{*}\cdot((V_{2}X_{2}^{*})^{\top}\odot(V_{3}X_{3}^{*})^{\top})-A_{1}
and
S
have at most
\beta_{2}\geq 1
contraction on
V_{1}
in the
\ell_{p}
case. If
\widehat{X}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},\widehat{X}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},\widehat{X}_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|SV_{1}\widehat{X}_{1}\otimes V_{2}\widehat{X}_{2}\otimes V_{3}\widehat{X}_{3}-SA\|_{p}^{p}\leq\beta\underset{X_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}}{\min}\|SV_{1}X_{1}\otimes V_{2}X_{2}\otimes V_{3}X_{3}-SA\|_{p}^{p},

where
\beta\geq 1
,
then

\displaystyle\|V_{1}\widehat{X}_{1}\otimes V_{2}\widehat{X}_{2}\otimes V_{3}\widehat{X}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}\lesssim\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\beta\min_{X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}}\|V_{1}X_{1}\otimes V_{2}X_{2}\otimes V_{3}X_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}.

The proof is essentially the same as the proof of Theorem
D.7
:

Proof.

Let
A,V_{1},V_{2},V_{3},S,X_{1}^{*},X_{2}^{*},X_{3}^{*},\beta_{1},\beta_{2}
be as stated in the theorem. Let

\widehat{X}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},\widehat{X}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},\widehat{X}_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|SV_{1}\widehat{X}_{1}\otimes V_{2}\widehat{X}_{2}\otimes V_{3}\widehat{X}_{3}-SA\|_{p}^{p}\leq\beta\underset{X_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}}{\min}\|SV_{1}X_{1}\otimes V_{2}X_{2}\otimes V_{3}X_{3}-SA\|_{p}^{p}.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
D.7
, we have,

\displaystyle\|SV_{1}\widehat{X}_{1}\otimes V_{2}\widehat{X}_{2}\otimes V_{3}\widehat{X}_{3}-SA\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle=\penalty\

\displaystyle 2^{2-2p}\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}\|V_{1}\widehat{X}_{1}\otimes V_{2}\widehat{X}_{2}\otimes V_{3}\widehat{X}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}-(2^{1-p}\frac{1}{\beta_{2}}+\beta_{1})\|V_{1}X^{*}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{*}_{2}\otimes V_{3}X^{*}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}

The only difference from the proof of Theorem
D.7
is that instead of using triangle inequality, we actually use
\|x+y\|_{p}^{p}\leq 2^{p-1}\|x\|_{p}^{p}+\|y\|_{p}^{p}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\|V_{1}\widehat{X}_{1}\otimes V_{2}\widehat{X}_{2}\otimes V_{3}\widehat{X}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle 2^{2p-2}\beta_{2}\|SV_{1}\widehat{X}_{1}\otimes V_{2}\widehat{X}_{2}\otimes V_{3}\widehat{X}_{3}-SA\|_{p}^{p}+(2^{p-1}+2^{2p-2}\beta_{1}\beta_{2})\|V_{1}X^{*}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{*}_{2}\otimes V_{3}X^{*}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle 2^{2p-2}\beta_{2}\beta\|SV_{1}X^{*}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{*}_{2}\otimes V_{3}X^{*}_{3}-SA\|_{p}^{p}+(2^{p-1}+2^{2p-2}\beta_{1}\beta_{2})\|V_{1}X^{*}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{*}_{2}\otimes V_{3}X^{*}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle 2^{2p-2}\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\beta\|V_{1}X^{*}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{*}_{2}\otimes V_{3}X^{*}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}+(2^{p-1}+2^{2p-2}\beta_{1}\beta_{2})\|V_{1}X^{*}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{*}_{2}\otimes V_{3}X^{*}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle 2^{p-1}\beta(1+2\beta_{1}\beta_{2})\|V_{1}X^{*}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{*}_{2}\otimes V_{3}X^{*}_{3}-A\|_{p}^{p}.

∎

Lemma E.6
.

Let
\min(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\geq k
. Given three matrices
V_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times b_{1}}
,
V_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times b_{2}}
, and
V_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times b_{3}}
, there exists an algorithm which takes
O(\nnz(A))+n\poly(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})
time and outputs a tensor
C\in\mathbb{R}^{c_{1}\times c_{2}\times c_{3}}
and three matrices
\widehat{V}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{c_{1}\times b_{1}}
,
\widehat{V}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{c_{2}\times b_{2}}
and
\widehat{V}_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{c_{3}\times b_{3}}
with
c_{1}=c_{2}=c_{3}=\poly(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})
, such that with probability
0.99
, for any
\alpha\geq 1
, if
X^{\prime}_{1},X^{\prime}_{2},X^{\prime}_{3}
satisfy that,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\widehat{V}_{1}X_{1}^{\prime})_{i}\otimes(\widehat{V}_{2}X_{2}^{\prime})_{i}\otimes(\widehat{V}_{3}X_{3}^{\prime})_{i}-C\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\underset{X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}}{\min}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\widehat{V}_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes(\widehat{V}_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes(\widehat{V}_{3}X_{3})_{i}-C\right\|_{p}^{p},

then,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(V_{1}X_{1}^{\prime})_{i}\otimes(V_{2}X_{2}^{\prime})_{i}\otimes(V_{3}X_{3}^{\prime})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\lesssim\alpha\min_{X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}({V}_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes({V}_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes({V}_{3}X_{3})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}.

Proof.

For simplicity, we define
\OPT
to be

\displaystyle\min_{X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}({V}_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes({V}_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes({V}_{3}X_{3})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}.

Let
T_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{c_{1}\times n}
correspond to sampling according to the
\ell_{p}
Lewis weights of
V_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times b_{1}}
, where
c_{1}=\widetilde{b_{1}}
. Let
T_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{c_{2}\times n}
be sampling according to the
\ell_{p}
Lewis weights of
V_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times b_{2}}
, where
c_{2}=\widetilde{b_{2}}
. Let
T_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{c_{3}\times n}
be sampling according to the
\ell_{p}
Lewis weights of
V_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times b_{3}}
, where
c_{3}=\widetilde{b_{3}}
.

For any
\alpha\geq 1,
let
X^{\prime}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X^{\prime}_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\|T_{1}V_{1}X^{\prime}_{1}\otimes T_{2}V_{2}X^{\prime}_{2}\otimes T_{3}V_{3}X^{\prime}_{3}-A(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3})\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\penalty\

\displaystyle\alpha\min_{X_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}}\|T_{1}V_{1}X_{1}\otimes T_{2}V_{2}X_{2}\otimes T_{3}V_{3}X_{3}-A(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3})\|_{p}^{p}.

First, we regard
T_{1}
as the sketching matrix for the remainder. Then by Lemma D.11 in
[
SWZ17
]
and Theorem
D.7
, we have

\displaystyle\|V_{1}X^{\prime}_{1}\otimes T_{2}V_{2}X^{\prime}_{2}\otimes T_{3}V_{3}X^{\prime}_{3}-A(I,T_{2},T_{3})\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\alpha\min_{X_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}}\|V_{1}X_{1}\otimes T_{2}V_{2}X_{2}\otimes T_{3}V_{3}X_{3}-A(I,T_{2},T_{3})\|_{p}^{p}.

Second, we regard
T_{2}
as the sketching matrix for
V_{1}X_{1}\otimes V_{2}X_{2}\otimes T_{3}V_{3}X_{3}-A(I,I,T_{3})
. Then by Lemma D.11 in
[
SWZ17
]
and Theorem
D.7
, we have

\displaystyle\|V_{1}X^{\prime}_{1}\otimes V_{2}X^{\prime}_{2}\otimes T_{3}V_{3}X^{\prime}_{3}-A(I,I,T_{3})\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\penalty\

\displaystyle\alpha\min_{X_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{1}\times k},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{2}\times k},X_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{b_{3}\times k}}\|V_{1}X_{1}\otimes V_{2}X_{2}\otimes T_{3}V_{3}X_{3}-A(I,I,T_{3})\|_{p}^{p}.

Third, we regard
T_{3}
as the sketching matrix for
V_{1}X_{1}\otimes V_{2}X_{2}\otimes V_{3}X_{3}-A
. Then by Lemma D.11 in
[
SWZ17
]
and Theorem
D.7
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(V_{1}X_{1}^{\prime})_{i}\otimes(V_{2}X_{2}^{\prime})_{i}\otimes(V_{3}X_{3}^{\prime})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\lesssim\alpha\min_{X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}({V}_{1}X_{1})_{i}\otimes({V}_{2}X_{2})_{i}\otimes({V}_{3}X_{3})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}.

∎

E.4
Solving small problems

Combining Section B.5 in
[
SWZ17
]
and the proof of Theorem
D.4
, for any
p=a/b
with
a,b
are integers, we can obtain the
\ell_{p}
version of Theorem
D.4
.

E.5
Bicriteria algorithm

We present several bicriteria algorithms with different tradeoffs. We first present an algorithm that runs in nearly linear time and outputs a solution with rank
\widetilde{O}(k^{3})
in Theorem
E.7
. Then we show an algorithm that runs in
\nnz(A)
time but outputs a solution with rank
\poly(k)
in Theorem
E.8
. Then we explain an idea which is able to decrease the cubic rank to quadratic, and thus we can obtain Theorem
E.9
.

Theorem E.7
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
, let
r=\widetilde{O}(k^{3})
. There exists an algorithm which takes
\nnz(A)\cdot\widetilde{O}(k)+n\poly(k)+\poly(k)
time and outputs three matrices
U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}U_{i}\otimes V_{i}\otimes W_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\widetilde{O}(k^{3-p/2})\log^{3}n\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A_{k}}\|A_{k}-A\|_{p}^{p}

holds with probability
9/10
.

Proof.

We first choose three dense Cauchy transforms
S_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{i}}
. According to Section
B.7
, for each
i\in[3]
,
A_{i}S_{i}
can be computed in
\nnz(A)\cdot\widetilde{O}(k)
time. Then we apply Lemma
E.6
. We obtain three matrices
Y_{1}=T_{1}A_{1}S_{1},Y_{2}=T_{2}A_{2}S_{2},Y_{3}=T_{3}A_{3}S_{3}
and a tensor
C=A(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3})
. Note that for each
i\in[3]
,
Y_{i}
can be computed in
n\poly(k)
time. Because
C=A(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3})
and
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\widetilde{O}(k)}
are three sampling and rescaling matrices,
C
can be computed in
\nnz(A)+\widetilde{O}(k^{3})
time. At the end, we just need to run an
\ell_{p}
-regression solver to find the solution for the problem:

\displaystyle\min_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times s_{2}\times s_{3}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{s_{3}}X_{i,j,l}(Y_{1})_{i}\otimes(Y_{2})_{j}\otimes(Y_{3})_{j}\right\|_{p}^{p},

where
(Y_{1})_{i}
denotes the
i
-th column of matrix
Y_{1}
. Since the size of the above problem is only
\poly(k)
, this can be solved in
\poly(k)
time.
∎

Theorem E.8
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
, let
r=\widetilde{O}(k^{15})
. There exists an algorithm that takes
\nnz(A)+n\poly(k)+\poly(k)
time and outputs three matrices
U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}U_{i}\otimes V_{i}\otimes W_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\poly(k,\log n)\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A_{k}}\|A_{k}-A\|_{p}^{p}

holds with probability
9/10
.

Proof.

We first choose three sparse
p
-stable transforms
S_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{i}}
. According to Section
B.7
, for each
i\in[3]
,
A_{i}S_{i}
can be computed in
O(\nnz(A))
time. Then we apply Lemma
E.6
, and can obtain three matrices
Y_{1}=T_{1}A_{1}S_{1},Y_{2}=T_{2}A_{2}S_{2},Y_{3}=T_{3}A_{3}S_{3}
and a tensor
C=A(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3})
. Note that for each
i\in[3]
,
Y_{i}
can be computed in
n\poly(k)
time. Because
C=A(T_{1},T_{2},T_{3})
and
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\widetilde{O}(k)}
are three sampling and rescaling matrices,
C
can be computed in
\nnz(A)+\widetilde{O}(k^{3})
time. At the end, we just need to run an
\ell_{p}
-regression solver to find the solution to the problem,

\displaystyle\min_{X\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{1}\times s_{2}\times s_{3}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{s_{3}}X_{i,j,l}(Y_{1})_{i}\otimes(Y_{2})_{j}\otimes(Y_{3})_{l}-C\right\|_{p}^{p},

where
(Y_{1})_{i}
denotes the
i
-th column of matrix
Y_{1}
. Since the size of the above problem is only
\poly(k)
, it can be solved in
\poly(k)
time.
∎

Theorem E.9
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, let
r=\widetilde{O}(k^{2})
. There exists an algorithm which takes
\nnz(A)\cdot\widetilde{O}(k)+n\poly(k)+\poly(k)
time and outputs three matrices
U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}U_{i}\otimes V_{i}\otimes W_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\widetilde{O}(k^{3-1.5p})\log^{3}n\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A_{k}}\|A_{k}-A\|_{p}^{p}

holds with probability
9/10
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to Theorem
D.14
.
∎

Algorithm 31

\ell_{p}
-Low Rank Approximation, Bicriteria Algorithm,

-
\widetilde{O}(k^{2})
, Input Sparsity Time

(

)
\triangleright

s_{1}\leftarrow s_{2}\leftarrow s_{3}\leftarrow\widetilde{O}(k)
.

For each
i\in[3]
, choose
S_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times s_{i}}
to be the composition of a sparse

\triangleright

,

.

procedure

As for
\ell_{1}
, notice that if we first apply a sparse Cauchy transform, we can reduce the rank of the matrix to
\poly(k)
. Theyn we can apply a dense Cauchy transform and further reduce the dimension, while only incurring another
\poly(k)
factor in the approximation ratio. By combining sparse
p
-stable and dense
p
-stable transforms, we can improve the running time from
\nnz(A)\cdot\widetilde{O}(k)
to be
\nnz(A)
by losing some additional
\poly(k)
factors in the approximation ratio.

Corollary E.10
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
,
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, let
r=\widetilde{O}(k^{2})
. There exists an algorithm which takes
\nnz(A)+n\poly(k)+\poly(k)
time and outputs three matrices
U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}U_{i}\otimes V_{i}\otimes W_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\poly(k,\log n)\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A_{k}}\|A_{k}-A\|_{p}^{p}

holds with probability
9/10
.

E.6
Algorithms

In this section, we show two different algorithms by using different kind of sketches. One is shown in Theorem
E.11
which gives a fast running time. Another one is shown in Theorem
E.12
which gives the best approximation ratio.

Theorem E.11
.

Given a
3
rd tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
, there exists an algorithm which takes
O(\nnz(A))+n\poly(k)+2^{\widetilde{O}(k^{2})}
time and outputs three matrices
U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
such that,

\displaystyle\left\|U\otimes V\otimes W-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\poly(k,\log n)\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{p}^{p}.

holds with probability at least
9/10
.

Proof.

First, we apply part (II) of Theorem
E.2
. Then
A_{i}S_{i}
can be computed in
O(\nnz(A))
time. Second, we use Lemma
E.6
to reduce the size of the objective function from
O(n^{3})
to
\poly(k)
in
n\poly(k)
time by only losing a constant factor in approximation ratio. Third, we use Claim
B.15
to relax the objective function from entry-wise
\ell_{p}
-norm to Frobenius norm, and this step causes us to lose some other
\poly(k)
factors in approximation ratio. As a last step, we use Theorem
C.45
to solve the Frobenius norm objective function.
∎

Theorem E.12
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
, there exists an algorithm that takes
n^{\widetilde{O}(k)}2^{\widetilde{O}(k^{3})}
time and output three matrices
U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
such that,

\displaystyle\|U\otimes V\otimes W-A\|_{p}^{p}\leq\widetilde{O}(k^{3-1.5p})\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{p}^{p}.

holds with probability at least
9/10
.

Proof.

First, we apply part (III) of Theorem
E.2
. Then, guessing
S_{i}
requires
n^{\widetilde{O}(k)}
time. Second, we use Lemma
E.6
to reduce the size of the objective from
O(n^{3})
to
\poly(k)
in polynomial time while only losing a constant factor in approximation ratio. Third, we solve the small optimization problem.
∎

E.7
CURT decomposition

Theorem E.13
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, let
k\geq 1
, and let
U_{B},V_{B},W_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
denote a rank-
k
,
\alpha
-approximation to
A
. Then there exists an algorithm which takes
O(\nnz(A))+O(n^{2})\poly(k)
time and outputs three matrices
C\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times c}
with columns from
A
,
R\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
with rows from
A
,
T\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times t}
with tubes from
A
, and a tensor
U\in\mathbb{R}^{c\times r\times t}
with
\rank(U)=k
such that
c=r=t=O(k\log k\log\log k)
, and

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{c}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{t}U_{i,j,l}\cdot C_{i}\otimes R_{j}\otimes T_{l}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\widetilde{O}(k^{3-1.5p})\alpha\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{p}^{p}

holds with probability
9/10
.

Proof.

We define

\displaystyle\OPT:=\min_{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{p}^{p}.

We already have three matrices
U_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
V_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
W_{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and these three matrices provide a
\rank
-
k
,
\alpha
approximation to
A
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}(U_{B})_{i}\otimes(V_{B})_{i}\otimes(W_{B})_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\alpha\OPT.

(46)

Let
B_{1}=V_{B}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
denote the matrix where the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
(V_{B})_{i}\otimes(W_{B})_{i}
. By Section B.3 in
[
SWZ17
]
, we can compute
D_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
which is a sampling and rescaling matrix corresponding to the Lewis weights of
B_{1}^{\top}
in
O(n^{2}\poly(k))
time, and there are
d_{1}=O(k\log k\log\log k)
nonzero entries on the diagonal of
D_{1}
. Let
A_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{2}}
denote the matrix obtained by flattening
A
along the
i
-th direction, for each
i\in[3]
.

Define
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|UB_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}
,
\widehat{U}=A_{1}D_{1}(B_{1}D_{1})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
V_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|V\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}
, and
U^{\prime}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|UB_{1}D_{1}-A_{1}D_{1}\|_{p}^{p}
.

By Claim
B.13
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}B_{1}D_{1}-A_{1}D_{1}\|_{p}^{p}\leq d_{1}^{1-p/2}\|U^{\prime}B_{1}D_{1}-A_{1}D_{1}\|_{p}^{p}.

Due to Lemma E.11 and Lemma E.8 in
[
SWZ17
]
, with constant probability, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{U}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}\leq d_{1}^{1-p/2}\alpha_{D_{1}}\|U^{*}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p},

(47)

where
\alpha_{D_{1}}=O(1)
.

Recall that
(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
denotes the matrix where the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
\widehat{U}_{i}\otimes(W_{B})_{i}
,
\forall i\in[k]
. Now, we can show,

\displaystyle\|V_{0}\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{U}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }V_{0}=\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|V\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top})-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ d_{1}^{1-p/2}\|U^{*}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}

by Equation (
47
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ d_{1}^{1-p/2}\|U_{B}B_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }U^{*}=\underset{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|UB_{1}-A_{1}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O(d_{1}^{1-p/2})\alpha\OPT.

by Equation (
46
)

(48)

We define
B_{2}=\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot W_{B}^{\top}
. We can compute
D_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
which is a sampling and rescaling matrix corresponding to the
\ell_{p}
Lewis weights of
B_{2}^{\top}
in
O(n^{2}\poly(k))
time, and there are
d_{2}=O(k\log k\log\log k)
nonzero entries on the diagonal of
D_{2}
.

Define
V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution of
\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|VB_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}
,
\widehat{V}=A_{2}D_{2}(B_{2}D_{2})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
W_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution of
\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|W\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}
, and
V^{\prime}
to be the optimal solution of
\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|VB_{2}D_{2}-A_{2}D_{2}\|_{p}^{p}
.

By Claim
B.13
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{V}B_{2}D_{2}-A_{2}D_{2}\|_{p}^{p}\leq d_{2}^{1-p/2}\|V^{\prime}B_{2}D_{2}-A_{2}D_{2}\|_{p}^{p}.

Due to Lemma E.11 and Lemma E.8 in
[
SWZ17
]
, with constant probability, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{V}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}\leq d_{2}^{1-p/2}\alpha_{D_{2}}\|V^{*}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p},

(49)

where
\alpha_{D_{2}}=O(1)
.

Recall that
(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
denotes the matrix for which the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
\widehat{U}_{i}\otimes\widehat{V}_{i}
,
\forall i\in[k]
. Now, we can show,

\displaystyle\|W_{0}\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|\widehat{V}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }W_{0}=\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|W\cdot(\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ d_{2}^{1-p/2}\|V^{*}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}

by Equation (
49
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ d_{2}^{1-p/2}\|V_{0}B_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }V^{*}=\underset{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|VB_{2}-A_{2}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O((d_{1}d_{2})^{1-p/2})\alpha\OPT.

by Equation (
E.7
)

(50)

We define
B_{3}=\widehat{U}^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top}
. We can compute
D_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
which is a sampling and rescaling matrix corresponding to the
\ell_{p}
Lewis weights of
B_{3}^{\top}
in
O(n^{2}\poly(k))
time, and there are
d_{3}=O(k\log k\log\log k)
nonzero entries on the diagonal of
D_{3}
.

Define
W^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution to
\min_{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|WB_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}
,
\widehat{W}=A_{3}D_{3}(B_{3}D_{3})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
,
and
W^{\prime}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\|WB_{3}D_{3}-A_{3}D_{3}\|_{p}^{p}
.

By Claim
B.13
, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}B_{3}D_{3}-A_{3}D_{3}\|_{p}^{p}\leq d_{3}^{1-p/2}\|W^{\prime}B_{3}D_{3}-A_{3}D_{3}\|_{p}^{p}.

Due to Lemma E.11 and Lemma E.8 in
[
SWZ17
]
, with constant probability, we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}\leq d_{3}^{1-p/2}\alpha_{D_{3}}\|W^{*}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p},

(51)

where
\alpha_{D_{3}}=O(1)
. Now we can show,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ d_{3}^{1-p/2}\|W^{*}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p},

by Equation (
51
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ d_{3}^{1-p/2}\|W_{0}B_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p},

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }W^{*}=\underset{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\|WB_{3}-A_{3}\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ O((d_{1}d_{2}d_{3})^{1-p/2})\alpha\OPT.

by Equation (
E.7
)

Thus, it implies,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\widehat{U}_{i}\otimes\widehat{V}_{i}\otimes\widehat{W}_{i}-A\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\poly(k,\log n)\OPT.

where
\widehat{U}=A_{1}D_{1}(B_{1}D_{1})^{\dagger}
,
\widehat{V}=A_{2}D_{2}(B_{2}D_{2})^{\dagger}
,
\widehat{W}=A_{3}D_{3}(B_{3}D_{3})^{\dagger}
.

∎

Appendix F
Robust Subspace Approximation (Asymmetric Norms for Arbitrary Tensors)

Recently,
[
CW15b
]
and
[
CW15a
]
study the linear regression problem and low-rank approximation problem under M-Estimator loss functions. In this section, we extend the matrix version of the low rank approximation problem to tensors, i.e., in particular focusing on tensor low-rank approximation under M-Estimator norms. Note that M-Estimators are very different from Frobenius norm and Entry-wise
\ell_{1}
norm, which are symmetric norms. Namely, flattening the tensor objective function along any of the dimensions does not change the cost if the norm is Frobenius or Entry-wise
\ell_{1}
-norm. However, for M-Estimator norms, we cannot flatten the tensor along all three dimensions. This property makes the tensor low-rank approximation problem under M-Estimator norms more difficult. This section can be split into two independent parts. Section
F.2
studies the
\ell_{1}
-
\ell_{2}
-
\ell_{2}
norm setting, and Section
F.3
studies the
\ell_{1}
-
\ell_{1}
-
\ell_{2}
norm setting.

F.1
Preliminaries

Definition F.1

(Nice functions for
M
-Estimators,
{\cal M}_{2}
,
{\cal L}_{p}
,
[
CW15a
]
)
.

We say an
M
-Estimator is
nice
if
M(x)=M(-x)
,
M(0)=0
,
M
is non-decreasing in
|x|
, there is a constant
C_{M}>0
and a constant
p\geq 1
so that for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
with
a\geq b
, we have

\displaystyle C_{m}\frac{|a|}{|b|}\leq\frac{M(a)}{M(b)}\leq(\frac{a}{b})^{p},

and also that
M(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}
is subadditive, that is,
M(x+y)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq M(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}+M(y)^{\frac{1}{p}}
.

Let
{\cal M}_{2}
denote the set of such nice
M
-estimators, for
p=2
. Let
{\cal L}_{p}
denote
M
-Estimators with
M(x)=|x|^{p}
and
p\in[1,2)
.

F.2
\ell_{1}
-Frobenius (a.k.a
\ell_{1}
-
\ell_{2}
-
\ell_{2}
) norm

Section
F.2.1
presents basic definitions and facts for the
\ell_{1}
-
\ell_{2}
-
\ell_{2}
norm setting. Section
F.2.2
introduces some useful tools. Section
F.2.3
presents the “no dilation” and “no contraction” bounds, which are the key ideas for reducing the problem to a “generalized” Frobenius norm low rank approximation problem. Finally, we provide our algorithms in Section
F.2.6
.

F.2.1
Definitions

We first give the definition for the
v
-norm of a tensor, and then give the definition of the
v
-norm for a matrix and a weighted version of the
v
-norm for a matrix.

Definition F.2

(Tensor
v
-norm)
.

For an
n\times n\times n
tensor
A
, we define the
v
-norm of
A
, denoted
\|A\|_{v}
, to be

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(\|A_{i,*,*}\|_{F})\right)^{1/p},

where
A_{i,*,*}
is the
i
-th face of
A
(along the
1
st direction), and
p
is a parameter associated with the function
M()
, which defines a nice
M
-Estimator.

Definition F.3

(Matrix
v
-norm)
.

For an
n\times d
matrix
A
, we define the
v
-norm of
A
, denoted
\|A\|_{v}
, to be

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}M(\|A_{i,*}\|_{2})^{1/p},

where
A_{i,*}
is the
i
-th row of
A
, and
p
is a parameter associated with the function
M()
, which defines a nice
M
-Estimator.

Definition F.4
.

Given matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
, let
A_{i,*}
denote the
i
-th row of
A
. Let
T_{S}\subset[n]
denote the indices
i
such that
e_{i}
is chosen for
S
. Using a probability vector
q
and a sampling and rescaling matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
from
q
, we will estimate
\|A\|_{v}
using
S
and a re-weighted version,
\|S\cdot\|_{v,w^{\prime}}
of
\|\cdot\|_{v}
, with

\displaystyle\|SA\|_{v,w^{\prime}}=\left(\sum_{i\in T_{S}}w_{i}^{\prime}M(\|A_{i,*}\|_{2})\right)^{1/p},

where
w_{i}^{\prime}=w_{i}/q_{i}
. Since
w^{\prime}
is generally understood, we will usually just write
\|SA\|_{v}
. We will also need an “entrywise row-weighted” version :

\displaystyle|||SA|||=\left(\sum_{i\in T_{S}}\frac{w_{i}}{q_{i}}\|A_{i,*}\|_{M}^{p}\right)^{1/p}=\left(\sum_{i\in T_{S},j\in[d]}\frac{w_{i}}{q_{i}}M(A_{i,j})\right)^{1/p},

where
A_{i,j}
denotes the entry in the
i
-th row and
j
-th column of
A
.

Fact F.5
.

For
p=1
, for any two matrices
A
and
B
, we have
\|A+B\|_{v}\leq\|A\|_{v}+\|B\|_{v}
. For any two tensors
A
and
B
, we have
\|A+B\|_{v}\leq\|A\|_{v}+\|B\|_{v}
.

F.2.2
Sampling and rescaling sketches

Note that Lemmas 42 and 44 in
[
CW15a
]
are stronger than stated. In particular, we do not need to assume
X
is a square matrix. For any
m\geq z
, if
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
, then we have the same result.

Lemma F.6

(Lemma 42 in
[
CW15a
]
)
.

Let
\rho>0
and integer
z>0
. For sampling matrix
S
, suppose for a given
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with failure probability
\delta
it holds that
\|SAy\|_{M}=(1\pm 1/10)\|Ay\|_{M}
. There is
K_{1}=O(z^{2}/C_{M})
so that with failure probability
\delta(K_{\cal N}/C_{M})^{(1+p)d}
, for a constant
K_{\cal N}
, any rank-
z
matrix
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
has the property that if
\|AX\|_{v}\geq K_{1}\rho
, then
\|SAX\|_{v}\geq\rho
, and that if
\|AX\|_{v}\leq\rho/K_{1}
, then
\|SAX\|_{v}\leq\rho
.

Lemma F.7

(Lemma 44 in
[
CW15a
]
)
.

Let
\delta,\rho>0
and integer
z>0
. Given matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
, there exists a sampling and rescaling matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with
r=O(\gamma(A,M,w)\epsilon^{-2}dz^{2}\log(z/\epsilon)\log(1/\delta))
nonzero entries such that, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for any
\rank
-
z
matrix
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
, we have either

\displaystyle\|SAX\|_{v}\geq\rho,

or

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon)\|AX\|_{v}-\epsilon\rho\leq\|SAX\|_{v}\leq(1+\epsilon)\|AX\|_{v}+\epsilon\rho.

Lemma F.8

(Lemma 43 in
[
CW15a
]
)
.

For
r>0
, let
\widehat{r}=r/\gamma(A,M,w)
, and let
q\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
have

\displaystyle q_{i}=\min\{1,\widehat{r}\gamma_{i}(A,M,w)\}.

Let
S
be a sampling and rescaling matrix generated using
q
, with weights as usual
w_{i}^{\prime}=w_{i}/q_{i}
. Let
W\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times z}
, and
\delta>0
. There is an absolute constant
C
so that for
\widehat{r}\geq Cz\log(1/\delta)/\epsilon^{2}
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle(1-\epsilon)\|AW\|_{v,w}\leq\|SAW\|_{v,w^{\prime}}\leq(1+\epsilon)\|AW\|_{v,w}.

F.2.3
No dilation and no contraction

Lemma F.9
.

Given matrices
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
,
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
, let
V^{*}=\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}}{\arg\min}\|UV-A\|_{v}
. If
S\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
has at most
c_{1}
-dilation on
UV^{*}-A
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\|S(UV^{*}-A)\|_{v}\leq c_{1}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v},

and it has at most
c_{2}
-contraction on
U
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\|SUx\|_{v}\geq\frac{1}{c_{2}}\|Ux\|_{v},

then
S
has at most
(c_{2},c_{1}+\frac{1}{c_{2}})
-contraction on
(U,A)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ \rank-k\penalty\ V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m},\|SUV-SA\|_{v}\geq\frac{1}{c_{2}}\|UV-A\|_{v}-(c_{1}+\frac{1}{c_{2}})\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}.

Proof.

Let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
,
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and
S\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
be the same as that described in the lemma. Let
(V-V^{*})_{j}
denote the
j
-th column of
V-V^{*}
. Then
\forall\penalty\ \rank-k\penalty\ V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\|SUV-SA\|_{v}\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|SUV-SUV^{*}\|_{v}-\|SUV^{*}-SA\|_{v}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|SUV-SUV^{*}\|_{v}-c_{1}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|SU(V-V^{*})\|_{v}-c_{1}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j=1}^{m}\|SU(V-V^{*})_{j}\|_{v}-c_{1}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{c_{2}}\|U(V-V^{*})_{j}\|_{v}-c_{1}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{c_{2}}\|UV-UV^{*}\|_{v}-c_{1}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{c_{2}}\|UV-A\|_{v}-\frac{1}{c_{2}}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}-c_{1}\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \frac{1}{c_{2}}\|UV-A\|_{v}-\left((\frac{1}{c_{2}}+c_{2})\|UV^{*}-A\|_{v}\right),

where the first inequality follows by the triangle inequality, the second inequality follows since
S
has at most
c_{1}
dilation on
UV^{*}-A
, the third inequality follows since
S
has at most
c_{2}
contraction on
U
, and the fourth inequality follows by the triangle inequality.
∎

Claim F.10
.

Given matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
, for any distribution
p=(p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{n})
define random variable
X
such that
X=\|A_{i}\|_{2}/p_{i}
with probability
p_{i}
where
A_{i}
is the
i
-th row of matrix
A
. Then take
m
independent samples
X^{1},X^{2},\cdots,X^{m}
, and let
Y=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}X^{j}
. We have

\displaystyle\Pr[Y\leq 1000\|A\|_{v}]\geq.999.

Proof.

We can compute the expectation of
X^{j}
, for any
j\in[m]
,

\displaystyle\E[X^{j}]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\|A_{i}\|_{2}}{p_{i}}\cdot p_{i}=\|A\|_{v}.

Then
\E[Y]=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\E[X^{j}]=\|A\|_{v}
. Using Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Pr[Y\geq\|A\|_{v}]\leq.001.

∎

Lemma F.11
.

For any fixed
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and
\rank
-
k

V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
with
d=\poly(k)
, there exists an algorithm that takes
\poly(n,d)
time to compute a sampling and rescaling diagonal matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with
s=\poly(k)
nonzero entries such that, with probability at least
.999
, we have: for all
\rank
-
k

V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\|U^{*}V^{*}-U^{*}V\|_{v}\lesssim\|SU^{*}V^{*}-SU^{*}V\|_{v}\lesssim\|U^{*}V^{*}-U^{*}V\|_{v}.

Lemma F.12

(No dilation)
.

Given matrices
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
,
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
with
d=\poly(k)
, define
V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
to be the optimal solution
\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}}{\min}\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}
. Choose a sampling and rescaling diagonal matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with
s=\poly(k)
according to Lemma
F.8
. Then with probability at least
.99
, we have: for all
\rank
-
k

V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\|SU^{*}V-SA\|_{v}\lesssim\|U^{*}V^{*}-U^{*}V\|_{v}+O(1)\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}\lesssim\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}.

Proof.

Using Claim
F.10
and Lemma
F.11
, we have with probability at least
.99
, for all
\rank
-
k

V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|SU^{*}V-SA\|_{v}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|SU^{*}V-SU^{*}V^{*}\|_{v}+\|SU^{*}V^{*}-SA\|_{v}

by triangle inequality

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|SU^{*}V-SU^{*}V^{*}\|_{v}+O(1)\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}

by Claim
F.10

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|U^{*}V-U^{*}V^{*}\|_{v}+O(1)\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}

by Lemma
F.11

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}+\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}+O(1)\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}

by triangle inequality

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\penalty\ \|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}.

∎

Lemma F.13

(No contraction)
.

Given matrices
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
,
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
with
d=\poly(k)
, define
V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
to be the optimal solution
\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}}{\min}\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}
. Choose a sampling and rescaling diagonal matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with
s=\poly(k)
according to Lemma
F.8
. Then with probability at least
.99
, we have: for all
\rank
-
k

V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}\lesssim\|SU^{*}V-SA\|_{v}+O(1)\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}.

Proof.

This follows by Lemma
F.9
, Claim
F.10
and Lemma
F.12
.
∎

F.2.4
Oblivious sketches,
MSketch

In this section, we recall a concept called
M
-sketches for
M
-estimators which is defined in
[
CW15b
]
.
M
-sketch is an oblivious sketch for matrices.

Theorem F.14

(Theorem 3.1 in
[
CW15b
]
)
.

Let
\OPT
denote
\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|Ax-b\|_{G}
. There is an algorithm that in
O(\nnz(A))+\poly(d\log n)
time, with constant probability finds
x^{\prime}
such that
\|Ax^{\prime}-b\|_{G}\leq O(1)\OPT
.

Definition F.15

(M-Estimator sketches or
MSketch

[
CW15b
]
)
.

Given parameters
N,n,m,b>1
, define
h_{\max}=\lfloor\log_{b}(n/m)\rfloor
,
\beta=(b-b^{-h_{\max}})/(b-1)
and
s=Nh_{\max}
.
For each
p\in[n]
,
\sigma_{p},g_{p},h_{p}
are generated (independently) in the following way,

\displaystyle\sigma_{p}

\displaystyle\leftarrow\pm 1,

\displaystyle\mathrm{\penalty\ chosen\penalty\ with\penalty\ equal\penalty\ probability},

\displaystyle g_{p}

\displaystyle\in[N],

\displaystyle\mathrm{\penalty\ chosen\penalty\ with\penalty\ equal\penalty\ probability},

\displaystyle h_{p}

\displaystyle\leftarrow t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{\penalty\ chosen\penalty\ with\penalty\ probability\penalty\ }1/(\beta b^{t})\mathrm{\penalty\ for\penalty\ }t\in\{0,1,\cdots h_{\max}\}.

For each
p\in[n]
, we define
j_{p}=g_{p}+Nh_{p}
.
Let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
denote the scaling vector such that, for each
j\in[s]
,

\displaystyle w_{j}=\begin{cases}\beta b^{h_{p}},&\mathrm{if\penalty\ there\penalty\ exists\penalty\ }p\in[n]\mathrm{\penalty\ s.t.}j=j_{p},\\
0&\mathrm{\penalty\ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Let
\overline{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{Nh_{\max}\times n}
be such that, for each
j\in[s]
,for each
p\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\overline{S}_{j,p}=\begin{cases}\sigma_{p},&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }j=g_{p}+N\cdot h_{p},\\
0,&\mathrm{\penalty\ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Let
D_{w}
denote the diagonal matrix where the
i
-th entry on the diagonal is the
i
-th entry of
w
. Let
S=D_{w}\overline{S}
.
We say
(\overline{S},w)
or
S
is an
MSketch
.

Definition F.16

(Tensor
\|\|_{v,w}
-norm)
.

For a tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n_{1}\times n_{2}}
and a vector
w\in\mathbb{d}
, we define

\displaystyle\|A\|_{v,w}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}w_{i}\|A_{i,*,*}\|_{F}.

Let
(\overline{S},w)
denote an
MSketch
, and let
S=D_{w}\overline{S}
. If
v
corresponds to a scale-invariant M-Estimator, then for any three matrices
U,V,W
, we have the following,

\displaystyle\|(\overline{S}U)\otimes V\otimes W\|_{v,w}=\|(D_{w}\overline{S}U)\otimes V\otimes W\|_{v}=\|(SU)\otimes V\otimes W\|_{v}.

Fact F.17
.

For a tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, let
S\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
denote an
MSketch
(defined in
F.15
) with
s=\poly(k,\log n)
. Then
SA
can be computed in
O(\nnz(A))
time.

Lemma F.18
.

For any fixed
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and
\rank
-
k

V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
with
d=\poly(k)
, let
S\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
denote an
MSketch
(defined in Definition
F.15
) with
s=\poly(k,\log n)
rows. Then with probability at least
.999
, we have: for all
\rank
-
k

V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\|U^{*}V^{*}-U^{*}V\|_{v}\lesssim\|SU^{*}V^{*}-SU^{*}V\|_{v}\lesssim\|U^{*}V^{*}-U^{*}V\|_{v}.

Lemma F.19

(No dilation, Theorem 3.4 in
[
CW15b
]
)
.

Given matrices
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
,
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
with
d=\poly(k)
, define
V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}}{\min}\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}
. Choose an
MSketch

S\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
with
s=\poly(k,\log n)
according to Definition
F.15
. Then with probability at least
.99
, we have: for all
\rank
-
k

V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\|SU^{*}V-SA\|_{v}\lesssim\|U^{*}V^{*}-U^{*}V\|_{v}+O(1)\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}\lesssim\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}.

Lemma F.20

(No contraction)
.

Given matrices
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times m}
,
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
with
d=\poly(k)
, define
V^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
to be the optimal solution to
\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}}{\min}\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}
. Choose an
MSketch

S\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
with
s=\poly(k,\log n)
according to Definition
F.15
. Then with probability at least
.99
, we have: for all
\rank
-
k

V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
,

\displaystyle\|U^{*}V-A\|_{v}\lesssim\|SU^{*}V-SA\|_{v}+O(1)\|U^{*}V^{*}-A\|_{v}.

F.2.5
Running time analysis

Lemma F.21
.

Given a tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d\times d}
, let
S\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n}
denote an
MSketch
with
s
rows. Let
SA
denote a tensor that has size
s\times d\times d
. For each
i\in\{2,3\}
, let
(SA)_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times ds}
denote a matrix obtained by flattening tensor
SA
along the
i
-th dimension. For each
i\in\{2,3\}
, let
S_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{ds\times s_{i}}
denote a CountSketch transform with
s_{i}
columns. For each
i\in\{2,3\}
, let
T_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{t_{i}\times d}
denote a CountSketch transform with
t_{i}
rows. Then

\mathrm{(I)}
For each
i\in\{2,3\}
,
(SA)_{i}S_{i}
can be computed in
O(\nnz(A))
time.

\mathrm{(II)}
For each
i\in\{2,3\}
,
T_{i}(SA)_{i}S_{i}
can be computed in
O(\nnz(A))
time.

Proof.

Proof of Part (I). First note that
(SA)_{2}S_{2}
has size
n\times S_{2}
. Thus for each
i\in[d],j\in[s_{2}]
, we have,

\displaystyle((SA)_{2}S_{2})_{i,j}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{ds}((SA)_{2})_{i,x^{\prime}}(S_{2})_{x^{\prime},j}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }(SA)_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times ds},S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{ds\times s_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}((SA)_{2})_{i,(y-1)s+z}(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}(SA)_{z,i,y}(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}

by unflattening

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}\left(\sum_{x=1}^{n}S_{z,x}A_{x,i,y}\right)(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}\sum_{x=1}^{n}S_{z,x}\cdot A_{x,i,y}\cdot(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}.

For each nonzero entry
A_{x,i,y}
, there is only one
z
such that
S_{z,x}
is nonzero. Thus there is only one
j
such that
(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}
is nonzero. It means that
A_{x,i,y}
can only affect one entry of
((SA)_{2}S_{2})_{i,j}
.
Thus,
(SA)_{2}S_{2}
can be computed in
O(\nnz(A))
time. Similarly, we can compute
(SA)_{3}S_{3}
in
O(\nnz(A))
time.

Proof of Part (II). Note that
T_{2}(SA)_{2}S_{2}
has size
t_{2}\times s_{2}
. Thus for each
i\in[t_{2}],j\in[s_{2}]
, we have,

\displaystyle(T_{2}(SA)_{2}S_{2})_{i,j}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{x=1}^{d}\sum_{y^{\prime}=1}^{ds}(T_{2})_{i,x}((SA)_{2})_{x,y^{\prime}}(S_{2})_{y^{\prime},j}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }(SA)_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times ds}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{x=1}^{d}\sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}(T_{2})_{i,x}((SA)_{2})_{x,(y-1)s+z}(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{x=1}^{d}\sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}(T_{2})_{i,x}(SA)_{z,x,y}(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}

by unflattening

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{x=1}^{d}\sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}(T_{2})_{i,x}\left(\sum_{w=1}^{n}S_{z,w}A_{w,x,y}\right)(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{x=1}^{d}\sum_{y=1}^{d}\sum_{z=1}^{s}\sum_{w=1}^{n}(T_{2})_{i,x}\cdot S_{z,w}\cdot A_{w,x,y}\cdot(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}.

For each nonzero entry
A_{w,x,y}
, there is only one
z
such that
S_{z,w}
is nonzero. There is only one
i
such that
(T_{2})_{i,x}
is nonzero. Since there is only one
z
to make
S_{z,w}
nonzero, there is only one
j
, such that
(S_{2})_{(y-1)s+z,j}
is nonzero. Thus,
T_{2}(SA)_{2}S_{2}
can be computed in
O(\nnz(A))
time. Similarly, we can compute
T_{3}(SA)_{3}S_{3}
in
O(\nnz(A))
time.
∎

F.2.6
Algorithms

We first give a “warm-up” algorithm in Theorem
F.22
by using a sampling and rescaling matrix. Then we improve the running time to be polynomial in all the parameters by using an oblivious sketch, and thus we obtain Theorem
F.23
.

Theorem F.22
.

Given a
3
rd order tensor
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n\times n}
, for any
k\geq 1
, let
r=O(k^{2})
. There exists an algorithm which takes
n^{\poly(k)}
time and outputs three matrices
U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r}
such that

\displaystyle\|U\otimes V\otimes W-A\|_{v}\leq\poly(k)\underset{\rank-k\penalty\ A^{\prime}}{\min}\|A^{\prime}-A\|_{v},

holds with probability at least
9/10
.

Proof.

We define
\OPT
as follows,

\displaystyle\OPT=\underset{U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\left\|U\otimes V\otimes W-A\right\|_{v}=\underset{U,V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}U_{i}\otimes V_{i}\otimes W_{i}-A\right\|_{v}.

Let
A_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{2}}
denote the matrix obtained by flattening tensor
A
along the
1
st dimension. Let
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
denote the optimal solution. We fix
U^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
, and consider this objective function,

\displaystyle\min_{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\left\|U^{*}\otimes V\otimes W-A\right\|_{v}\equiv\min_{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\left\|U^{*}\cdot(V^{\top}\odot W^{\top})-A_{1}\right\|_{v},

(52)

which has cost at most
\OPT
, and where
V^{\top}\odot W^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times n^{2}}
denotes the matrix for which the
i
-th row is a vectorization of
V_{i}\otimes W_{i},\forall i\in[k]
. (Note that
V_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the
i
-th column of matrix
V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
).
Choose a sampling and rescaling diagonal matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
according to
U^{*}
, which has
s=\poly(k)
non-zero entries. Using
S
to sketch on the left of the objective function when
U^{*}
is fixed (Equation (
52
)), we obtain a smaller problem,

\displaystyle\min_{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{v}\equiv\min_{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\left\|SU^{*}\cdot(V^{\top}\odot W^{\top})-SA_{1}\right\|_{v}.

(53)

Let
V^{\prime},W^{\prime}
denote the optimal solution to the above problem, i.e.,

\displaystyle V^{\prime},W^{\prime}=\underset{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\arg\min}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{v}.

Then using properties (no dilation Lemma
F.12
and no contraction Lemma
F.13
) of
S
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|U^{*}\otimes V^{\prime}\otimes W^{\prime}-A\right\|_{v}\leq\alpha\OPT.

where
\alpha
is an approximation ratio determined by
S
.

By definition of
\|\cdot\|_{v}
and
\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\|\cdot\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\text{dim}}\|\cdot\|_{2}
, we can rewrite Equation (
53
) in the following way,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\left(\left((SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W\right)_{i,j,l}-(SA)_{i,j,l}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{s}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{s}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\left(\left((SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W\right)_{i,j,l}-(SA)_{i,j,l}\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sqrt{s}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{F}.

(54)

Given the above properties of
S
and Equation (
F.2.6
), for any
\beta\geq 1
, let
V^{\prime\prime},W^{\prime\prime}
denote a
\beta
-approximate solution of
\underset{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{F}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V^{\prime\prime}\otimes W^{\prime\prime}-SA\right\|_{F}\leq\beta\cdot\underset{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}{\min}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{F}.

(55)

Then,

\displaystyle\left\|U^{*}\otimes V^{\prime\prime}\otimes W^{\prime\prime}-A\right\|_{v}\leq\sqrt{s}\alpha\beta\cdot\OPT.

(56)

In the next few paragraphs we will focus on solving Equation (
55
). We start by fixing
W^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
to be the optimal solution of

\displaystyle\min_{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{F}.

We use
(SA)_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times ns}
to denote the matrix obtained by flattening the tensor
SA\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n\times n}
along the second direction. We use
Z_{2}=(SU^{*})^{\top}\odot(W^{*})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times ns}
to denote the matrix where the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
(SU^{*})_{i}\otimes W_{i}^{*}
. We can consider the following objective function,

\displaystyle\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|VZ_{2}-(SA)_{2}\|_{F}.

Choosing a sketching matrix
S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{ns\times s_{2}}
with
s_{2}=O(k/\epsilon)
gives a smaller problem,

\displaystyle\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|VZ_{2}S_{2}-(SA)_{2}S_{2}\|_{F}.

Letting
\widehat{V}=(SA)_{2}S_{2}(Z_{2}S_{2})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
, then

\displaystyle\|\widehat{V}Z_{2}-(SA)_{2}\|_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|VZ_{2}-(SA)_{2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|V((SU^{*})^{\top}\odot(W^{*})^{\top})-(SA)_{2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)\min_{V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W^{*}-SA\|_{F}

by unflattening

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)\min_{V,W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\|_{F}.

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ definition\penalty\ of\penalty\ }W^{*}

(57)

We define
D_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
to be a diagonal matrix obrained by copying the
n\times n
identity matrix
s
times on
n
diagonal blocks of
D_{2}
. Then it has
ns
nonzero entries. Thus,
D_{2}
also can be thought of as a matrix that has size
n^{2}\times ns
.

We can think of
(SA)_{2}S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}}
as follows,

\displaystyle(SA)_{2}S_{2}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (A(S,I,I))_{2}S_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{A_{2}}_{n\times n^{2}}\cdot\underbrace{D_{2}}_{n^{2}\times n^{2}}\cdot\underbrace{S_{2}}_{ns\times s_{2}}\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }D_{2}\text{\penalty\ can\penalty\ be\penalty\ thought\penalty\ of\penalty\ as\penalty\ having\penalty\ size\penalty\ }n^{2}\times ns

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ A_{2}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}c_{2,1}I_{n}&&&\\
&c_{2,2}I_{n}&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&c_{2,n}I_{n}\end{bmatrix}\cdot S_{2}

where
I_{n}
is an
n\times n
identity matrix,
c_{2,i}\geq 0
for each
i\in[n]
, and the number of nonzero
c_{2,i}
is
s
.

For the last step, we fix
SU^{*}
and
\widehat{V}
. We use
(SA)_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times ns}
to denote the matrix obtained by flattening the tensor
SA\in\mathbb{R}^{s\times n\times n}
along the third direction. We use
Z_{3}=(SU^{*})^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times ns}
to denote the matrix where the
i
-th row is the vectorization of
(SU^{*})_{i}\otimes\widehat{V}_{i}
. We can consider the following objective function,

\displaystyle\min_{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|WZ_{3}-(SA)_{3}\|_{F}.

Choosing a sketching matrix
S_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{ns\times s_{3}}
with
s_{3}=O(k/\epsilon)
gives a smaller problem,

\displaystyle\min_{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|WZ_{3}S_{3}-(SA)_{3}S_{3}\|_{F}.

Let
\widehat{W}=(SA)_{3}S_{3}(Z_{3}S_{3})^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
. Then

\displaystyle\|\widehat{W}Z_{3}-(SA)_{3}\|_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)\min_{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|WZ_{3}-(SA)_{3}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ property\penalty\ of\penalty\ }S_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)\min_{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|W((SU^{*})^{\top}\odot\widehat{V}^{\top})-(SA)_{3}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ definition\penalty\ }Z_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)\min_{W\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}}\|(SU^{*})\otimes\widehat{V}\otimes W-SA\|_{F}

by unflattening

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\penalty\ (1+\epsilon)^{2}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{F}.

by Equation (
F.2.6
)

We define
D_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
to be a diagonal matrix formed by copying the
n\times n
identity matrix
s
times on
n
diagonal blocks of
D_{3}
. Then it has
ns
nonzero entries. Thus,
D_{3}
also can be thought of as a matrix that has size
n^{2}\times ns
and
D_{3}
is uniquely determined by
S
.

Similarly as to the
2
nd dimension, for the
3
rd dimension, we can think of
(SA)_{3}S_{3}
as follows,

\displaystyle(SA)_{3}S_{3}=

\displaystyle\penalty\ (A(S,I,I))_{3}S_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{A_{3}}_{n\times n^{2}}\cdot\underbrace{D_{3}}_{n^{2}\times n^{2}}\cdot\underbrace{S_{3}}_{ns\times s_{3}}

\displaystyle\text{\penalty\ by\penalty\ }D_{3}\text{\penalty\ can\penalty\ be\penalty\ thought\penalty\ of\penalty\ as\penalty\ having\penalty\ size\penalty\ }n^{2}\times ns

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ A_{3}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}c_{3,1}I_{n}&&&\\
&c_{3,2}I_{n}&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&c_{3,n}I_{n}\end{bmatrix}\cdot S_{3}

where
I_{n}
is an
n\times n
identity matrix,
c_{3,i}\geq 0
for each
i\in[n]
and the number of nonzero
c_{3,i}
is
s
.

Overall, we have proved that,

\displaystyle\min_{X_{2},X_{3}}\|(SU^{*})\otimes(A_{2}D_{2}S_{2}X_{2})\otimes(A_{3}D_{3}S_{3}X_{3})-SA\|_{F}\leq(1+\epsilon)^{2}\left\|(SU^{*})\otimes V\otimes W-SA\right\|_{F},

(58)

where diagonal matrix
D_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
(with
ns
nonzero entries) and
D_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n^{2}\times n^{2}}
(with
ns
nonzero entries) are uniquely determined by diagonal matrix
S\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
(
s
nonzero entries). Let
X^{\prime}_{2}
and
X_{3}^{\prime}
denote the optimal solution to the above problem (Equation (
58
)). Let
V^{\prime\prime}=(A_{2}D_{2}S_{2}X_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
W^{\prime\prime}=(A_{3}D_{3}S_{3}X_{3}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|U^{*}\otimes V^{\prime\prime}\otimes W^{\prime\prime}-A\right\|_{v}\leq\sqrt{s}\alpha\beta\OPT.

We construct matrix
\widetilde{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}
by copying matrix
(SA)_{2}S_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}}

s_{3}
times,

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}=\begin{bmatrix}(SA)_{2}S_{2}&(SA)_{2}S_{2}&\cdots&(SA)_{2}S_{2}.\end{bmatrix}

(59)

We construct matrix
\widetilde{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}
by copying the
i
-th column of matrix
(SA)_{3}S_{3}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{3}}
into
(i-1)s_{2}+1,\cdots,is_{2}
columns of
\widetilde{W}
,

\displaystyle{\small\widetilde{W}=\begin{bmatrix}((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{1}\cdots((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{1}&((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{2}\cdots((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{2}&\cdots&((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{s_{3}}\cdots((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{s_{3}}.\end{bmatrix}}

(60)

Although we don’t know
S
, we can guess all of the possibilities. For each possibility, we can find a solution
\widetilde{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}
to the following problem,

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{3}}U_{(i-1)s_{3}+j}\otimes((SA)_{2}S_{2})_{i}\otimes((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{j}-A\right\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{3}}U_{(i-1)s_{3}+j}\cdot\vect(((SA)_{2}S_{2})_{i}\otimes((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{j})-A_{1}\right\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{3}}U_{(i-1)s_{3}+j}\cdot(\widetilde{V}^{\top}\odot\widetilde{W}^{\top})^{(i-1)s_{3}+j}-A_{1}\right\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}}\left\|U\cdot(\widetilde{V}^{\top}\odot\widetilde{W}^{\top})-A_{1}\right\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}}\left\|UZ-A_{1}\right\|_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \min_{U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}s_{3}}\|U^{i}Z-A_{1}^{i}\|_{2},

where the first step follows by flattening the tensor along the
1
st dimension,
U_{(i-1)s_{3}+j}
denotes the
(i-1)s_{3}+j
-th column of
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times s_{2}s_{3}}
,
A_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n^{2}}
denotes the matrix obtained by flattening tensor
A
along the
1
st dimension, the second step follows since
\widetilde{V}^{\top}\odot\widetilde{W}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{s_{2}s_{3}\in n^{2}}
is defined to be the matrix where the
(i-1)s_{3}+j
-th row is vectorization of
((SA)_{2}S_{2})_{i}\otimes((SA)_{3}S_{3})_{j}
, the fourth step follows by defining
Z
to be
\widetilde{V}^{\top}\odot\widetilde{W}^{\top}
, and the last step follows by definition of
\|\cdot\|_{v}
norm. Thus, we obtain a multiple regression problem and it can be solved directly by using
[
CW13
,
NN13
]
.

Finally, we take the best
\widetilde{U},\widetilde{V},\widetilde{W}
over all the guesses. The entire running time is dominated by the number of guesses, which is
n^{\poly(k)}
. This completes the proof.
∎
